## 3. Open questions

Rows are kept in the order they were opened. A row is *Answered* when a finding settles it, *Closed* when the project's direction made it moot (D18 in particular), and open rows carry their plan as of 2026-10-19.

| # | Question | Plan |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Q1 | *Answered 2026-09-30 → F23; closed 2026-10-08.* Yes: userptr BO mapped at GPU VA == CPU VA, zero copies; `munmap` under a mapping → F26. The coherence sub-question is answered in practice: guest memory is mapped as dma-bufs at the guest's addresses (F40, F55) and the game runs hours of play and 1.3 million submissions without a coherence fault (F59, F85). | — |
| Q2 | *Answered 2026-09-29 → F12; slots answered 2026-09-30 → F19:* `contextControl` is a CONTEXT_CONTROL packet, `cmds[0]` the state preamble IB, `cmds[1]` a 2-dword IB (NOP), `cmds[2]` unused in the first submit; the frame DCB comes through `0xc0188132`. | — |
| Q3 | *Answered 2026-09-30 → F21.* Yes: every compute dispatch preamble writes `COMPUTE_STATIC_THREAD_MGMT_SE0..3 = 0xffffffff`; graphics buffers write none. BC5's mask is ANDed into those writes. | — |
| Q4 | Which draw/CB/DB register usage is Sony-specific beyond public PM4? *First data 2026-09-30 → F21:* one unresolved SH offset (mm 0x2e80) and one APU-only opcode (0x8e). | *Parked 2026-10-08:* nothing left unresolved blocks ASTRO BOT (F85); reopens with the next title, whose captures extend `docs/formats/agc.md`. *Reopened 2026-10-09 by the second title:* compute-queue `DISPATCH_INDIRECT` with an address instead of an offset (F90, F91; public AMD form, new to this host), and a `LOAD_SH_REG_INDEX` that loads zero dwords, which the host's operand check takes for a null address (F92, F95; no-op, dropping it is harmless). *2026-10-19:* the SH offset 0x2e80 is the driver's "write nowhere" register (F111, F112; AnyPS5's name for it), and opcode 0x8e has not recurred. Open after the second title reached gameplay: the constant uconfig write 0xc383 (F208; forcing it to 0 changes nothing), the title's own `SET_BASE` [1,0,0] with small offsets ahead of compute-form `DISPATCH_INDIRECT`s (F209, TODO(verify) what the console's CP reads there), and the geometry-engine base the console's system programs (Q13). Plan: fold the three into `docs/formats/agc.md` as the independently written AGC driver of Q14 is specified from the public packet set; each one is settled by a run that reproduces the console's value, never by assumption. |
| Q5 | Storage: how much of the SSD/Kraken dependency can a software prefetch/cache hide? *Partly answered 2026-10-06 → F51, F71:* the level load was the host's own lookups, not storage (240 s → 135 s → 25 s); the board's M.2 is idle at 16 MiB/s of 700+. Remaining: titles that lean on the hardware decompressor. *2026-10-08 → F81, F83, F84:* the load window is 18 s (from 24) with a parallel `bc5-mount`; what remains is the game's loaders waiting on each other. | *Closed 2026-10-08:* answered for this title (F84; the maintainer: loading runs very smoothly). A title that leans on the hardware decompressor is a new question when one is tried. |
| Q6 | *Answered 2026-09-28 → F10.* Remaining sub-question: do other producers (console-side compressors) deviate from the PS5PKGTool/MkPFS layout? | *Closed 2026-10-08:* a second container format (PS5 packages, F82) is read through the same tree; a producer that deviates fails loudly in `inspect`, which is the signal to look. |
| Q7 | *Answered 2026-09-29 → F13.* | — |
| Q8 | *Answered 2026-09-29 → F14; measured 2026-10-01 → F39, F39a.* All 40 CUs execute, at one rate each: compute throughput is linear in the mask bits 0–19 of the two shader-engine registers. Remaining: the graphics stages' 16-bit `CU_EN` — which of an engine's 20 CUs it can select. | *Parked 2026-10-08:* the game runs on all 40 CUs with the masks it writes; which CUs `CU_EN` selects matters only for CU partitioning, not for playing. *2026-10-10 → F97:* in the game, `CU_EN` bits 0–9 carry work and bits 10–15 none (0x03ff = 0xffff, 0x00ff 15 % slower), consistent with one bit per CU of a 10-CU shader array, TODO(verify) with a pixel-shader-bound probe. *Closed 2026-10-19:* F97 is the answer for play — bits 0–9 carry work, one per CU of a 10-CU shader array (TODO(verify) with a pixel-shader-bound probe) — and D5's 36/4 partition is in no play setting: both titles run on all 40 CUs (F97; 36 CUs changed nothing for the hang of F184 either), so which CUs `CU_EN` selects matters only if partitioning returns. |
| Q9 | *Answered 2026-10-19 → F107 (with F91, F96, F106).* The stops had three host causes: the title's compute-form indirect dispatches, never run (rewritten for the GFX ring, F91); GPU-only heaps never imported (F96); and the tree refit started before its DMA copy and on lists its builders had not filled, since the host dropped dispatches with stale-looking arguments (F104, F106, F107). With the CP checking indirect arguments and a CP DMA wait before such a dispatch the runs end clean (six of six in experiment 0053, every run of 0122). What came after — the New Game hang and the night scene's fault — is Q11/Q13. *Partly answered 2026-10-09 → F91:* the compute-form indirect dispatches now run and the stop at ~210 s is gone in two runs of three. Remaining: why program 0x909939900 hangs the single ring, and the graphics-side fault of run 84. *2026-10-09 → F92:* the remaining faults are shader loads from the title's heap addresses, some with junk in bits 40–47; a dropped `LOAD_SH_REG_INDEX` precedes two stops. *→ F95:* that packet is a no-op; the stops follow a game state near 211 s, fault in guest holes in 0x12b0000000–0x12d0000000 and end in the title's own null read; trusting stale dispatch arguments does not help. *→ F96:* GPU-only heaps were never imported (fixed for this title); the stops remain about one in four, at wild addresses. Original question: why does the second title stop after some minutes (F89, F90)? A GPU fault at an address no mapping explains; after the reset the compute queue's jobs take 81 s to fail and the title stops submitting. Its compute IBs' compute-form indirect dispatches have never run here. | — |
| Q10 | *Answered 2026-10-12 → F108:* the menu does show over the scene; what was missing were the scene's `DRAW_INDIRECT` / multi draws, which the policy dropped (with them, the foreground and sharp menu texts); the maintainer moves through the menus with the controller, and the sound plays since the 12-channel port is mixed down. Original question: should the second title's menu show over its 3D backdrop? | *Closed 2026-10-12;* the indirect draws themselves are Q11. |
| Q11 | *Answered in part 2026-10-19 → F112, F173, F186, F207.* The title runs with `DRAW_INDIRECT`, the multi-draw packets and its code mapped again under the 0x80 byte (F112, F118); the reflection traversal (0x11d049aa00) and the BVH neighbour-link compaction that feeds it (0x909974400) are skipped in its launchers (F173, F207), so the scene has no ray-traced reflections and the opening and gameplay run (F207, F212). The traversal is the hardware `image_bvh_intersect_ray` loop over per-frame trees that are correct (F184, F185); with every query missing nothing hangs (F186). Original question: Can the second title run with its indirect draws? With them it stops at its first scene change: its shaders call function pointers carrying 0x80 in bits 47:40 (F109, F110); with the code mapped again under the byte the fault is gone and the GPU hangs instead, and a hang without a fault can take the machine down. Does the PS5 GPU ignore VA bits 47:40 (TODO(verify)), and what hangs after the aliased call? | Still open, two parts. (a) VA bits 47:40: the aliases work and no public source says the PS5 GPU ignores the bits (F92, F109, F110); stays a TODO(verify) until a public source or a measurement on a console says so, and the aliases stay. (b) Why the traversal loops and the compaction's output makes the geometry engine read address 0: that is Q13 — the two skips are diagnostics until it is answered. *2026-10-21:* the address-0 reads were the TF ring (F248); the traversal does not loop but is halted at its first stack push, whose FLAT address uses the console's apertures (F249); with `BC5_DIRECT_FLAT_APERTURE=1` everything runs without a skip in idle runs, but in play the GPU still hangs: a compute-only buffer of the reflection pipeline ran with a wrong program address (instruction fetch at 0x80'0000005000), once taking the machine down, and the reflections look blocky (F250, F251). Open: where that program address comes from, the blocky reflections, and only then the launchers' switch (the maintainer's call). *2026-10-22 → F253:* the address was another amdgpu client's compute program, left on the shared GFX ring in front of a compute-only buffer that inherits the title's binding from its previous buffer. It is a host defect of the shared ring, not of the reflections. A compute-state prefix (`BC5_DIRECT_CS_PREFIX`, off by default) is built and waits for its GPU run. The "blocky reflections" are the older water patchwork (F251, exp 0194). *2026-10-22 → F254:* 17 minutes of play with `=1` (skips kept) ran without a fault. Before it goes into the launchers, an A/B in the same scenes must show whether it lengthens the long GPU stretches. *2026-10-22 → F268, F275, F277:* with `BC5_DIRECT_AMM_PRT=1` the play with nothing skipped stays up but at half the frame rate (F268); the traversals cost nothing measurable in the frozen lake view (F275), and in play with only the compaction 0x909974400 skipped the title runs at the reflections-off rate (F277). Open: whether the reflections are drawn correctly with the compaction skipped, the compaction's own cost and heap writes, and the launchers' setting (the maintainer's call). |
| Q12 | *(2026-10-19)* **The second title's frame time.** It asks for 60 fps (flip rate 0, VSYNC) and gets a variable 30–42 in gameplay because its render thread spends three quarters of its time in the host's per-submission path, ~130 buffers a frame (F210; the audio clock and the flip label are console-correct, F211, F212). Submission chains per ioctl (ADR 0010, F213) cut the kernel submissions by 39 % but not the render thread's frame; with `COND_EXEC` segment-local and the game thread's chain held across its ioctls (ADR 0010 §A/§B, `BC5_DIRECT_CHAIN=2`) the night scene runs at 45 flips/s instead of 33, 1,658 instead of 4,463 kernel submissions a second, the render thread 93 → 83 % (F218, experiment 0165, verdict pending). | Steps (1) and (2) of experiment 0163's verdict are done (F218). Left, in experiment 0165's order: the hold bound of the chain watchdog (1 / 4 ms against 2), the ring thread's slices across polls, the `kyty-gpu` CPU rise with chains on (8 → 11–25 %), ASTRO BOT on chains, and the F208 bursts (Q13), survived in runs 803–805 and in no way settled by them. Whether `BC5_DIRECT_CHAIN=2` enters the play settings is the maintainer's call; the measurement that decides it is a 200 s gameplay run with the pad script against run 799. The inventory of what else the host does differently from the console is `docs/console-vs-host.md`. |
| Q13 | *(2026-10-19)* **The geometry engine's read from address 0** (F207, F208). At the first draws of the night scene, once the BVH compaction's output exists, the work distributor (client GE2) reads the first 64 KiB of GPU address space through a base register the console's system programs and the host leaves at 0; every readable WD/TF/ES/GS base reads 0 while the job is in flight, the first burst is survivable, a repeat hangs a job and resets the machine, and the kernel's reserved bottom 64 KiB cannot be mapped from user space. Which register, and who programs it on the console? | Two routes, both the maintainer's: root-only `umr -w` of the WD buffer-base candidates to a mapped buffer, one candidate per run, at the power switch; or the driver initialisation the console performs and the host stubs — which the independently written AGC driver of Q14 will have to own anyway, so the two questions are one piece of work. Until then: the zero pages above 64 KiB (kept, generic) and the skips of 0x909974400 and 0x11d049aa00 (diagnostic) in the launchers. |
| Q14 | *(2026-10-19, D18)* **Phasing the system-module pack out of the launchers.** `SHADPS4_SYSMODULES_PACK_DIR` names a directory whose `.sprx` take precedence over the host's own libraries (F17). Today it supplies, from a game dump's `fakelib/` (never firmware): `libSceAgc.sprx` and `libSceAgcDriver.sprx` — the LLE pair that builds the console-format command stream (F18, F19, D11) — and `libScePlayGo.sprx` and `libScePsml.sprx`, loaded since experiment 0010 with their use untraced; `libSceAmpr.sprx` left the pack in experiment 0035 (the host's own AMPR, F87–F89). ASTRO BOT's launcher points at its own `fakelib/`, the second title's at a copy of it without `libSceAmpr` (`sysmodules-pack-hle-ampr`), and `play-any.sh` passes the rule on. | (1) PlayGo and Psml: journal which of their exports the two titles import (the loader's stub journal, F188/F189's method), check what KytyPlus's tables already answer (TODO(verify): neither name is in patch 0001), cover the rest in the host, remove the two files from the pack and watch both titles. (2) The AGC pair: an independently written `libSceAgc`/`libSceAgcDriver` in the host — the public PM4 set the direct path already filters and executes (`docs/formats/agc.md`, F19–F21, F27–F42), the `/dev/gc` ioctl layouts of F12, the driver initialisation the LLE modules perform (56 queue registrations, the submit page, the named flexible areas: F19) — ported from AnyPS5's GPL-2.0 AGC library and recorder driver where its behaviour is its own (`docs/ports/anyps5-review.md` §1), with a packet-by-packet comparison against the LLE stream of the same frames (`bc5-agc report`) as the gate. (3) Then the variable leaves `bc5-run.env`, `play-pkg.sh`, `play-astro.sh` and `play-any.sh`, and the README no longer asks for the two modules from a dump. ADR 0012. |
| Q15 | *(2026-10-19)* **60 fps in ASTRO BOT's later levels.** The first level is at 59–60 fps (F99, F100); a later level at 46.5 (F126), its frame a latency chain of the game's own threads and the GPU (F120), the host's share of the draw thread 4.9 ms, and the submission cannot leave that thread (F121). Why the title's GPU timestamps read low unscaled is a TODO(verify) (F100). | The chains of Q12 apply to its draw thread as well (F218's §A/§B are generic); measure the later level with `BC5_DIRECT_CHAIN=2`, with the pad script of experiment 0061 for the same scene. |
| Q16 | *(2026-10-19)* **The four AnyPS5 ports are unverified on the box** (F214–F217: rumble from the vibration ports without an audio sink, NpManager's signed-out callbacks, three NpTrophy2 exports, SaveData search order, patterns and block accounting). They compiled nowhere but a syntax check. | Build with `tools/scripts/bc5-build.sh` (the live tree on the box is still the pre-port patch plus the menu work, experiment 0164's note), then the second title through a save and a delete under `KYTY_BC5_SAVE_TRACE=1`, its Np calls under the loader's journal, and a DualShock 4 (no audio sink) for the rumble; ASTRO BOT once for regressions. Each becomes a finding with a run number or is reverted. |
| Q17 | *(2026-10-19)* **ATRAC9 channel configuration 7** (F200): every ATRAC9 stream of the second title uses it, no public decoder knows it, the host plays them as silence. | The only lead is KytyPS5's name for these layouts, "vibration": compare one such stream's timing against the title's haptic events in a play session (`KYTY_BC5_INPUT_TRACE=1`, the audio trace of F211). If they are haptic tracks, silence is the right answer and the question closes; if they are audio, it stays open until a format note can be derived from the streams themselves. |
| Q18 | *(2026-10-19)* **Presentation without a copy** (F129–F131): KytyPlus copies and detiles the flipped 33 MB buffer before showing it (2.9 ms, of which ~1–1.2 could go); leaving the display buffer's flip-label wait to the GPU took the machine down twice (F130). | Low priority: not on any title's critical path today. A custom path (detile on the GPU into the swapchain image) only after Q12 and Q15, and only with a pinned label page and a bounded flip read. |

## 4. Architecture (summary)

```
PS5 game (eboot.bin, PRX)  →  KytyPlus host: loader, libkernel and every system library written independently (HLE; D18, ADR 0012)
                               ├ today: the game's own libSceAgc/libSceAgcDriver, LLE-loaded from a game dump's fakelib/ (D11; the pack, Q14)
                               └ next:  an independently written AGC driver in the host (Q14)
                            →  /dev/gc boundary  →  BC5 backend (direct path, ADR 0005/0006)  →  amdgpu/libdrm  →  BC-250
                                                     ├→ I/O layer (bc5-mount: .ffpfsc and .pkg, parallel reads, decode-ahead, disk cache)
                                                     └→ audio + input (host: PipeWire, SDL; haptics as audio to the DualSense's own USB device)
```

One host, one track: RPCSX is a reference for the `/dev/gc` layouts and the PM4 synchronisation packets (F12), not a host (D18). The backend's host interface is the `/dev/gc` boundary (F12, F18): submit ioctls, GPU memory mapping (`mmap`, flexible/tool memory), `kevent` completion, EOP flip and buffer labels. Whatever builds the command stream behind that boundary — the LLE pair today, the host's own driver tomorrow — gets the same backend.

Backend modes: (1) validation — decode + log, submit nothing (the captures of experiments 0011–0012); (2) hybrid — never built; (3) direct — own libdrm client, the guest's memory mapped 1:1 as dma-bufs of the host's backing files (ADR 0006), the packets filtered and rewritten for `amdgpu`'s command processor (the context-state stack, the label hand-overs, CP DMA waits: F34–F42), jobs in flight on the GFX ring with the title's asynchronous compute on the BC-250's compute rings (ADR 0009, F177–F181), graphics pieces behind a full-state prefix at cross-queue waits (ADR 0008), a register-state prefix before drawing buffers against state inherited from the desktop (F204), and submission chains behind `BC5_DIRECT_CHAIN` (ADR 0010, F213, F218). Everything that submits is behind `BC5_GC_MODE=direct` and `BC5_DIRECT_STAGE` (hard rule 5).

CU policy: D5's 36/4 split exists as a switch (`BC5_DIRECT_CU_MASK`, ANDed with the masks the titles write, F39/F39a) and is in no play setting; both titles run on all 40 CUs (F97, Q8).

## 5. Phases and gates

See `docs/PHASES.md`. Order as run: 0a `bc5-mount` → 1b KytyPlus + LLE `libSceAgc` (D11) → 1 AGC validation → 2 native shaders → 3 IB submit; gates G0a, G1b, G1, G2 and G3 are passed, all on this one track. Phases 0b (RPCSX baseline) and 4 (the backend inside RPCSX) named a host that boots the console's system software and are retired by D18 (ADR 0012); what follows phase 3 is the work of §3 (Q12–Q14), not a new phase.

## 7. Decisions taken so far

- D1 Project name **BC5** (BC-250 + PS5). Repo `bc5`; fallbacks `bc5-native`, `bc5-agc` if taken.
- D2 License GPL-2.0 (RPCSX compatibility).
- D3 Public, English, anti-piracy: no Sony material, bring your own console and dumps, no warranty.
- D4 Start with the input layer (`bc5-mount`) before anything GPU-related.
- D5 36/4 CU split as a backend switch, measured in phase 3.
- D6 Tools in Rust, backend in C++20 (RPCSX is C++).
- D7 (2026-09-29) Phase-1 preparation that needs no hardware and no game (Q2 research in the RPCSX sources, `docs/formats/agc.md`, the `bc5-agc` PM4 decoder with hand-built streams) may proceed before gate G0a is recorded, because it does not depend on `bc5-mount`. Phase 0b, anything under `backend/` and anything that submits to the GPU still wait for their gates. Maintainer's decision; overrides the "one phase at a time" rule of `CLAUDE.md` for this case only.
- D10 (2026-09-29) Prosper (github.com/mattias800/prosper, commit `1c93ab8`) is used **locally on the dev box as an external capture tool** while no firmware dump exists: it runs ASTRO BOT to its title screen without PS5 system modules. Prosper has **no licence**, so none of its code, text or data enters this repository, and nothing derived from it is distributed. It is built from source in the `ubuntu` distrobox (`~/src/prosper`, build in `~/bc5-work/prosper-build`) with one **local-only** patch (`~/bc5-work/prosper-bc5-dcb-dump.local.patch`, 24 lines in `hle_agc.cpp`) that writes each submitted DCB as raw dwords to `$BC5_AGC_DUMP_DIR`; its own `PROSPER_SHADER_DUMP` provides shader binaries. Captures live in `~/bc5-data/captures` and are never committed; experiments record only statistics produced by our tools. Caveat for every result: part of each DCB is written by Prosper's HLE of `sceAgcDcb*` rather than Sony's library, so captures describe "game + Prosper", not a console byte stream. Maintainer's decision.
- D11 (2026-09-30) **The host runtime becomes pluggable; track B is opened.** Following F16–F18: (1) the BC5 backend gets its host interface at the `/dev/gc` boundary (submit ioctls, `mmap` of GPU memory, flexible/tool memory, `kevent`, EOP flip, buffer labels), so any host that provides those calls can drive it. (2) Track A — RPCSX with the maintainer's own firmware dump — stays the reference path and keeps gate G0b. (3) Track B — KytyPlus (GPL-2.0-only, licence-compatible) with the game's own `fakelib/libSceAgc.sprx` and `libSceAgcDriver.sprx` LLE-loaded from the maintainer's dump. First task: implement in KytyPlus the 29 imports of experiment 0010 so that Sony's driver initialises, and route the `/dev/gc` ioctls to a capture buffer; that yields the first DCB built by Sony's code. KytyPlus changes are kept as patches under `backend/kytyplus-patches/` and may be offered upstream as PRs. (4) Phase 1 no longer depends on firmware: its captures come from track B. Prosper (D10) is retired as a capture source. Maintainer's decision, approved in chat ("ok - działaj, zatwierdzam").
- D9 (2026-09-29) Phase-2 preparation (ADR 0004: PM4 builder, memset IB, `dispatch-min` with `--dump-ib`/`--info`) may proceed before gates G0b and G1, because phase 0b is blocked on the maintainer's firmware dump and the preparation submits nothing. Running `dispatch-min --submit` still needs the maintainer's explicit go-ahead with the box idle, each time. *2026-09-30: first go-ahead used; experiment 0008 passed (phase 2 task 2 done), one encoder bug found and fixed on the way.*
- D8 (2026-09-29) BIOS memory split: **512 MiB VRAM carve-out** (amdgpu: VRAM 512M, GTT 7596M; Linux sees 14 GiB) instead of the 8 GiB carve-out recorded in experiment 0003. Rationale: the PS5 memory model is unified and phases 2–3 map guest memory as system RAM (userptr/GTT, Q1); RPCSX needs host RAM for the 16 GB guest address space. Risk: Vulkan paths that insist on a large device-local heap; if VSH boot (phase 0b) fails on memory, compare splits in a dedicated experiment.
- D12 (2026-10-01) **`ttm.pages_limit=3407872` (13 GiB) on the dev box's kernel command line**, set by the maintainer after runs 96–99 (F44–F47): amdgpu now reports 13,312 MiB of GTT instead of 7,597 (`mem_info_gtt_total` = pages limit × 4096 before and after, which settles the `TODO(verify)` of F44 by measurement). Rationale: the game reserves 12.4 GiB of direct memory and a draw's BO list names all of it that holds data. Risk: with 15.2 GiB of RAM, pinned game memory leaves little for the rest of the system; undo with `rpm-ostree kargs --delete=ttm.pages_limit=3407872`.
- D13 (2026-10-06) **After a cold boot the BC-250 comes up with 6 cores / 12 threads; a warm reboot is needed for 8 / 16.** The 8-core unlock service sets an SMU mask that a warm reset keeps and a power cycle loses, and it only re-arms the mask for the next reboot. Boots of 2026-10-02 08:43 and 2026-10-06 16:11 ran on 12 processors (run 117 and the pad tests); the boots of 2026-10-01 had 16. Check with `nproc` before a measurement; the fix is one reboot. Source: `journalctl -b <n> | grep smpboot` and the unlock script's own comment.
- D14 (2026-10-06) **Controller mapping is a text file, not an environment variable per case.** KytyPlus had `--keymap Control=Input` (keyboard, mouse, pad buttons as sources for the host-input stream) but no way to disable a physical pad input and no file. Patch 0001 adds `--input-config <file>` / `KYTY_BC5_INPUT_CONFIG` with the same grammar plus `Pad:<button|axis>=none`, pad axes as sources (`Pad:righttrigger`, `Pad:leftx-`) and `DefaultKeys=on|off`; the built-in keyboard layout stays unless a line takes a key or a control from it. The maintainer's immediate case (a pad whose R2 is stuck: `Pad:righttrigger=none`, `R2=O`) is two lines of `~/bc5-work/input.cfg`, named from `bc5-run.env`. Reference: `backend/kytyplus-patches/input.example.cfg`. 2026-10-08: the same file is edited from a menu in the game window (F2; `settingsOverlay.cpp`, ImGui in the presentation pass beside the IME keyboard, the simplest version first as decided with the maintainer): bind by pressing the input, pad inputs to ignore, the built-in layout, Apply live, Save to the file. The map holds the live configuration behind one mutex and is replaced whole on Apply; a bad line leaves it untouched and is shown. Confirmed by the maintainer in the game on 2026-10-08: F2 opens it, Bind takes the pressed input.
- D15 (2026-10-09) **Per-title settings for the direct path, in the title's launcher, not in the shared `bc5-run.env`.** A title's GPU heaps (`BC5_DIRECT_EAGER_NAMES`, by the names the title gives them) and its learned-fault file (`BC5_DIRECT_LEARNED`) belong to that title: the second title's learned windows had been written into ASTRO BOT's file (51 entries, removed). The second title's launchers are `run-pkg.sh` and `play-pkg.sh` (experiment 0037), the latter behind a desktop shortcut.
- D17 (2026-10-19) **The emulator window has a menu bar; the host still mounts nothing** (ADR 0011, experiment 0164). An ImGui menu bar (File: Open game image / Open package / Recent / Close session / Quit; Emulation: Pause, Restart, Settings, Fullscreen; Help: About) drawn in the settings overlay's pass, `kyty_emulator --menu` for the empty window with a launcher page, F1 or the pad's PS button during play. Opening and closing are exit codes like the F2 menu's 75: 76 = open the path in `$KYTY_BC5_MENU_DIR/choice.txt`, 77 = close the session; `play-any.sh` on the dev box loops over them and hands a `.pkg` to `play-pkg.sh` and a `.ffpfsc` to `play-astro.sh`, so the mount, the system-module pack and the per-title switches stay where D15 put them.
- D18 (2026-10-19) **The host does not use a PS5 firmware or system-software dump, and never will; every system library is written independently in the host** (ADR 0012). After the work of experiments 0154–0164 it is clear the project will not take the RPCSX road (booting the console's system modules): everything both titles needed was written in the host from public symbol names and the titles' own calls, following KytyPlus's model, and that is the rule from here. Track A of D11, phase 0b and phase 4 are retired; RPCSX stays a reference for the AGC/GPU side only (§6). The maintainer's own decrypted system-software root (`~/bc5-data/ps5-fw`) is private reference data: nothing is loaded or derived from it. The system-module pack (`SHADPS4_SYSMODULES_PACK_DIR`), today a copy of a game dump's `fakelib/` — `libSceAgc`, `libSceAgcDriver` (the LLE pair of D11), `libScePlayGo`, `libScePsml` — is to be phased out of the launchers as the host's own libraries cover what it provides, PlayGo and Psml first, the AGC pair last with an independently written driver (Q14). Maintainer's decision, stated 2026-10-19.
- D16 (2026-10-09) **Conversions that make the GPU execute work it never executed are off by default and tried one short run at a time, with the build synced to disk first.** Run 64 (F90) showed that such a change can take the machine down, and that a hard reset loses what was written just before it (the backend library came back empty). The compute-form `DISPATCH_INDIRECT` conversion stays behind `BC5_DIRECT_MEC_INDIRECT=1`; the second title's launchers turn it on with its one hanging program skipped (F91), after it ran clean in short, watched runs.
